Section Il. DESCRIPTION AND DATA

3. Purpose and Use                                      applied to correct for changes in cable loss which
                                                        occur with changes in temperature. In addition,
   a. Telephone Repeater AN/TCC-11 (fig. 1) is a        a means of ringing, talking, and listening to
four-wire, unattended, carrier-telephone repeater.      attended points is provided over an unamplified
It is used to extend the length of a 12-channel         voice-frequency maintenance circuit called the
carrier-telephone system that uses Telephone Ter-       order wire.
minals AN/TCC-7 and Telephone Repeaters
AN/TCC-8 at the attended points, and uses non-             Note. Throughout this technical manual, whenever the
loaded spiral-four cable as the main transmission       term AN/TCC-11 is used, it will refer to Telephone
medium. The extension obtained for each AN/             Repeater AN/TCC-11; similarly, reference to AN/TCC-—7
TCC-11 added to the system is 5%; miles (23 reels       will mean Telephone Terminal AN/TCC-7; reference to
of Cable Assembly CX—1065/G).                           TS-712 Test Set will mean Telephone Test Set TS—
                                                        712/TCC-11; and reference to AN/TCC-8 will mean
   b. Telephone Repeater AN/TCC-11 contains             Telephone Repeater AN/TCC-8. Basic nomenclature
facilities for amplifying, equalizing, and regulating   followed by ( ) refers to all models of the equipment,
the two-way transmission of a band of frequencies       regardless of past or present procurement.
from 12 ke (kilocycles) to 99 ke. This band con-
tains 12 carrier frequency message channels, a          4. System Application
68-ke pilot frequency, and three fault finding fre-            (fig. 2)
quencies of 83 ke, 91 ke, and 99 ke. Amplification
is supplied to overcome cable losses in the two            a. Block Diagram. 'The simplified block dia-
directions of transmission. Equalization is sup-        gram of figure 2 illustrates a typical application of
plied to correct for differences in cable loss at the   Telephone Repeater AN/TCC-11 in a carrier
various frequencies transmitted. Regulation is          communications system. For more complete sys-
                                                        tem application information, see TM 11-2150.

peratures from —65° F. to 150° F. The following       perature of the ambient air. This prevents con-
paragraphs contain instructions for minimizing        densation of moisture due to the high relative
the effect of unusual operating conditions.           humidity in tropical areas. When an installation

and climatic conditions associated with cold          moisture on the equipment.
weather. The following precautions should be

                            CHAPTER 5
    THEORY OF TELEPHONE REPEATER AN/TCC-I1

                                   Section |. INTRODUCTION

92. General                                             a. Transmission Circuits (par. 103). Trans-
                                                     mission circuits 1 and 2 perform identical functions
   Telephone Repeater AN/TCC-11 is an un-            of carrier-frequency signal amplification and order
attended carrier telephone repeater that amplifies,  wire signal transmission (without amplification)
equalizes, and regulates carrier-frequency signals   through the repeater. Transmission circuit 1
transmitted over systems using Telephone Terminal    operates in a direction opposite to that of trans-
AN/TCC-7 and Telephone Repeater AN/TCC-8             mission circuit 2.
at the attended points. The carrier-frequency
signals are amplified, equalized, and regulated at      b. Fault-locating Circuit (par. 123). A fault-
intervals of 534 miles of spiral-four cable to com-  locating circuit enables an attendant at a remote
pensate for attenuation produced by the cable.       terminal or attended repeater to check the opera-
Order wire voice signals are transmitted through     tions of Telephone Repeater AN/TCC-11.
the repeater in two directions without amplifica-
tions, equalization, or regulation. Repeater oper-      c. Power Distributing Circuit (par. 125). A
ation can be tested from attended points by trans-   power distributing circuit provides the proper
mission of fault-locating frequencies from those     operating voltages for the circuits of Telephone
points. Repeater operation can be tested at the      Repeater AN/TCC-11.
repeater location by use of Telephone Test Set
TS-712/TCC-11. Connections on Telephone Test         95. Transmission Circuits
Set TS-712/TCC-11 permit maintenance person-
nel at the repeater location to talk over the order            (fig. 19)
wire circuit to attended points. Power to energize
the repeater circuits is supplied over the spiral-      Transmission circuits 1 and 2 each consist of an
four cable from attended Telephone Terminal          input circuit, an equalizing circuit, an amplifying
AN/TCC-7 or attended Telephone Repeater              circuit, a regulating circuit, an output circuit, and
AN/TCC-8.                                            an order wire circuit. Except for the directions of
                                                     transmission, the function of transmission circuits
93. Block Diagram                                    1 and 2 are identical.

                                                     1. The carrier-frequency signals (message chan-
peater AN/TCC-11. The repeater always is in-         nel, pilot, and fault-locating frequencies) are
stalled so that the fault-locating frequencies and   equalized, amplified, and passed to the outgoing
                                                     cable pair at the J2 end of the repeater. The order
power obtained from the attended point (Tele-        wire signals are transmitted unamplified and un-
phone Terminal AN/TCC-7 or Telephone Re-             equalized to the same outgoing cable pair. The
peater AN/TCC-8) are applied to the repeater         100 mils de is used for the operation of the repeater.
through the Jl end. The designations J1 and J2       Paragraphs 96 through 98 describe the functional
are embossed on the end cover assemblies. This       operation of transmission circuit 1.
relationship is indicated on the block diagram.

129. Locating and Protection Circuits
                                                      131. Lightning Protection
   The locating and protection circuits of Tele-
phone Repeater AN/TCC-11 include the open                         (fig. 29)
circuit locating system (par. 130) and the lightning
protection circuit (par. 131). The open circuit          Carbon block lightning arresters are provided
locating system provides a means of determining at    to protect Telephone Repeater AN/TCC-11 cir-
an attended point the location of a two-wire, power   cults from damage caused by surges induced in
interrupting cable break. The lightning protec-       the cable as a result of nearby lightning strokes.
tion circuit is used to divert to ground high         Lightning arresters E5, E6, E8, and E9 (300 v)
voltages induced by lightning or power line dis-      are bridged across the secondary windings of
turbances, thereby protecting the spiral-four cable
and the repeater equipment.                           repeating coils T1 and T2, and across the primary
                                                      windings of repeating coils T3 and T4. Lightning

    (fi. 56)  |                                       v) are connected between the center tap of the

                 CHAPTER 6
FIELD MAINTENANCE INSTRUCTIONS

Section |. TROUBLESHOOTING AT FIELD MAINTENANCE LEVEL

       Warning: When servicing Telephone Repeater AN/TCC-11, be extremely careful because of the
high voltages exposed. Keep one hand in the pocket when measuring socket voltages with the probe.
With the high voltage off, potentials as great as 600 volts still may be present in the equipment. Before
touching any part after the voltage is shut off, short the part to ground. Always disconnect the spiral-
four cable connectors and discharge capacitors C1, C2, C3, and C4 before making resistance measure-

ments.

132. Troubleshooting Procedures                          (2) Power circuit test. These measurements
                                                                (par. 140) prevent further damage to the
   The first step in servicing defective equipment             equipment from possible short circuits.
is to sectionalize the fault to the assembly respon-           Since this test gives an indication of the
sible for the abnormal operation of the system.                condition of the power circuits, its
The second step is to localize the fault. Local-               function is more than preventive.
ization means tracing the fault to the defective
part responsible for the abnormal condition.             (3) Operational tests. The operational tests
Some faults, such as burned-out resistors, often                (pars. 139 and 150) are important
can be located by sight, smell, and hearing. The               because they frequently indicate the

                                       CHAPTER 7

SHIPMENT AND LIMITED STORAGE AND DEMOLITION TO PREVENT
                                      ENEMY USE

     Section |. SHIPMENT AND LIMITED STORAGE

193. Disassembly                                     194. Repacking for Shipment or Limited
                                                           Storage
   The following instructions are recommended
as a guide for preparing Telephone Repeater             a. The exact procedure in repacking for ship-
AN/TCC-11 for transportation and storage.            ment or limited storage depends on the material
                                                     available and the conditions under which the
   a. Disconnect the spiral-four cable connectors    equipment is to be shipped or stored. Refer to
from receptacles J1 and J2.                          paragraph 22 and reverse the instructions given

   b. Thread the receptacle covers back on connec-   in that paragraph.
tors J1 and J2, and tighten the cover over TEST         b. Whenever practicable, place a dehydrating
receptacle J3, using the wingbolt. Be sure the
receptacle covers are clean before replacing them.   agent such as silica gel inside the cardboard
                                                     cartons. Protect the crates with a waterproof
   c. Wipe a small amount of silicone grease over    tape barrier. Seal the seams of the paper barrier
the O-shaped rubber gaskets on the CONTROLS          with waterproof sealing compound or tape. Pack
access port covers, replace the covers, and tighten  the protected cartons in a padded wooden case,
both wingnuts.                                       providing at least 3 inches of excelsior padding or
                                                     some similar material between the paper barrier
                                                     and the packing case.

     Section Il. DEMOLITION OF MATERIEL TO PREVENT ENEMY USE

195. General                                            b. Cut. Cut cords and wiring, using axes,
                                                     handaxes, or machetes.
   The demolition procedures outlined in paragraph
196 will be used to prevent the enemy from using        c. Burn. Burn cords, resistors, capacitors,
or salvaging this equipment. Demolition of the       coils, wiring, and technical manuals, using gasoline,
equipment will be accomplished only upon order       kerosene, oil, flame throwers, or incendiary
of the commander.                                    grenades.

196. Methods of Destruction                             d. Bend. Bend end and assembly panels,
                                                     chassis.
   a. Smash. Smash the controls, tubes, switches,
capacitors, transformers, and filter network, using     e. Explode. If explosives are necessary, use
sledges, axes, handaxes, pickaxes, hammers,          firearms, grenades, or TNT.
crowbars, or heavy tools.
                                                        f. Dispose. Bury or scatter the destroyed parts
                                                     in slit trenches, fox holes, or other holes, or throw
                                                     them into streams.

                                                        g. Destroy Everything.

                                          DEFINITIONS

A-b direction of transmission—Transmission in             Energizing circuit—The circuit and series of con-
                                                       tacts through which the winding of a relay is en-
the direction from the A terminal to the B termi-      ergized and the relay operated.

nal. Indicated on drawings from left to right.            Equalization—Compensation for variations in
                                                       attenuation with frequency in order to make the
Ambient temperature—Temperature of the air in          resultant transmission substantially constant for
                                                       all frequencies.
the immediate vicinity of equipment or parts.
                                                          Flat—Having the same loss at all frequencies.
Antising element—A circuit or part used to sup-        The part of the cable attenuation characteristic
                                                       which is constant with frequency.
press undesired oscillations.
                                                          Group input frequencies or group output fre-
Attenuation characteristic (loss characteristic)       quencies—Frequencies in the range from 12 ke
                                                       to 60 ke, which include twelve message channels
A curve showing the manner in which attenuation        adjacent to each other.

through a circuit, cable, or network. varies with         Junction point—An unattended repeater loca-
                                                       tion where the power circuits from the attended
frequency.                                             points are turned back (or looped back) to their
   Ba direction of transmission—Transmission in        sources.

the direction from the B terminal to the A termi-         Locking path—cirecuit and series of contacts
                                                       through which a relay is held operating after initial
nal. Indicated on drawings from right to left.         actuation.

Bulge—The portion of the cable attenuation                Message channel—One of the 12 v-f transmission
                                                       bands of 300 to 3,500 cycles bandwidth.
characteristic which is greatest at a frequency in
                                                          Modem—Contraction for “modulator-demodu-
the center of the transmitted frequency band.          lator.’’

Oable attenuation—Decrease in signal amplitude            Order wire—A 300 to 1,700 cycle v-f mainte-
                                                       nance circuit.
produced by the effects of the cable characteristics.
                                                          Pilot frequency—A 68-ke frequency used for
Cable building-out—The addition of loss with the       purposes of regulating the gain of the attended
                                                       amplifiers to keep their outputs constant and
same attenuation characteristic as lengths of cable,   independent of normal cable attenuation changes
                                                       in temperature.
obtained with adjustable networks.
                                                          Power loop—The path of the power circuit over
Cable system—A carrier communication system            the spiral-four cable from a power source at an
                                                       attended station to unattended repeaters and
which uses only spiral-four cable for interconnec-     back to the source.

tion of the terminals and repeater equipment.             Repeater—A combination of one or more am-
   Cable and radio system—A system which uses          plifiers and associated circuits spaced along the
                                                       spiral-four cable to compensate for cable attenua-
both spiral-four cable and radio sets for intercon-    tion.

necting the terminal and repeater equipment.              Repeating coil—A low-ratio transformer which is
   Carrier—A frequency with which a band of            introduced into a circuit to separate balanced from
                                                       unbalanced circuits, or to isolate repeater or
frequencies is modulated or demodulated.               terminal circuits from the line.

CHAN—Abbreviation for channel.                                                                                                 147
Channel output frequencies or channel input fre-

quencies—Frequencies in the range from 4 ke to 20

ke, including four message channels.

Circuit noise—Electrical disturbance brought to

the receiver from a telephone system, excluding
disturbance picked up acoustically by the handset

transmitter.

nel carrier system which directs the actions of the

system.            interference caused by energy

   Orosstalk— The
from one circuit

   Radio system—A system which has one or more        and spiraled around a polyethylene core. The

   Reference voltage—A constant voltage source

ing voltage, used for control purposes.               and a thermoplastic outer jacket.
